   {: .quote }

1. When companions took baby birds and their mother became distressed, Prophet Muhammad ﷺ ordered them to return the young. The same report records him objecting to the burning of an ant colony.

   > “Who has distressed this bird by taking her young? Return her young to her.” — *Sunan Abi Dawud* 2675, graded *sahih* [sound][4](#ref-4){: .footnote}[15](#ref-15){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ told prayer leaders to shorten communal prayer because those behind them could include the elderly, the sick, the weak, and people with other limitations.

   > “When one of you leads the people in prayer, let him shorten it, for among them are the weak, the sick, and the elderly.” — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 703[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ said that the best people are those who learn the Qur’an and teach it to others.

   > “The best of you are those who learn the Qur’an and teach it.” — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 5027[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ taught that true richness is not having many possessions but being inwardly content.

   > “Richness is not having many possessions. True richness is richness of the soul.” — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 6446[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ described health and free time as two blessings that many people fail to value properly.

   > “There are two blessings in which many people are deceived: health and free time.” — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 6412[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. When Prophet Muhammad ﷺ found wet grain hidden beneath dry grain in the market, he told the seller to expose the defect so buyers could see it and condemned deception.

   > “Why did you not put it on top so people could see it? Whoever deceives us is not of us.” — *Sahih Muslim* 102[3](#ref-3){: .footnote}[14](#ref-14){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. The Qur’an permits trade by mutual consent and forbids consuming other people’s wealth wrongfully.

   > "O believers! Do not devour one another's wealth illegally, but rather trade by mutual consent. And do not kill ˹each other or˺ yourselves. Surely Allah is ever Merciful to you." (**Sūrat al-Nisāʾ 4:29**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿يَـٰٓأَيُّهَا ٱلَّذِينَ ءَامَنُوا۟ لَا تَأْكُلُوٓا۟ أَمْوَٰلَكُم بَيْنَكُم بِٱلْبَـٰطِلِ إِلَّآ أَن تَكُونَ تِجَـٰرَةً عَن تَرَاضٍ مِّنكُمْ ۚ وَلَا تَقْتُلُوٓا۟ أَنفُسَكُمْ ۚ إِنَّ ٱللَّهَ كَانَ بِكُمْ رَحِيمًا ۝٢٩﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ النِّسَاءِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. The Qur’an instructs believers to honor their contracts and obligations.

   > "O believers! Honour your obligations. All grazing livestock has been made lawful to you—except what is hereby announced to you and hunting while on pilgrimage. Indeed, Allah commands what He wills." (**Sūrat al-Māʾidah 5:1**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿يَـٰٓأَيُّهَا ٱلَّذِينَ ءَامَنُوٓا۟ أَوْفُوا۟ بِٱلْعُقُودِ ۚ أُحِلَّتْ لَكُم بَهِيمَةُ ٱلْأَنْعَـٰمِ إِلَّا مَا يُتْلَىٰ عَلَيْكُمْ غَيْرَ مُحِلِّى ٱلصَّيْدِ وَأَنتُمْ حُرُمٌ ۗ إِنَّ ٱللَّهَ يَحْكُمُ مَا يُرِيدُ ۝١﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ المَائـِدَةِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. When a tax collector claimed that some property had been given to him as a personal gift, Prophet Muhammad ﷺ asked whether he would have received those gifts if he had simply stayed at home, exposing the conflict created by gifts tied to public office.

   > “Why did he not sit in his father’s or mother’s house and see whether gifts would be given to him?” — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 7174[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ prayed against leaders who make life unnecessarily hard for people and prayed for gentleness toward leaders who treat them gently.

   > “O God, whoever is put in charge of my community and is hard on them, be hard on him; whoever is gentle with them, be gentle with him.” — *Sahih Muslim* 1828a[3](#ref-3){: .footnote}[14](#ref-14){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. The Qur’an prohibited interest-based usury and distinguished it from ordinary trade. Whatever later jurists debate about the exact legal boundary of *riba* [prohibited increase], the stated moral direction is clear: debt cannot become an unlimited means of extracting wealth from another person.

   > "O believers! Fear Allah, and give up outstanding interest if you are ˹true˺ believers. If you do not, then beware of a war with Allah and His Messenger! But if you repent, you may retain your principal—neither inflicting nor suffering harm." (**Sūrat al-Baqarah 2:278-279**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿يَـٰٓأَيُّهَا ٱلَّذِينَ ءَامَنُوا۟ ٱتَّقُوا۟ ٱللَّهَ وَذَرُوا۟ مَا بَقِىَ مِنَ ٱلرِّبَوٰٓا۟ إِن كُنتُم مُّؤْمِنِينَ ۝٢٧٨ فَإِن لَّمْ تَفْعَلُوا۟ فَأْذَنُوا۟ بِحَرْبٍ مِّنَ ٱللَّهِ وَرَسُولِهِۦ ۖ وَإِن تُبْتُمْ فَلَكُمْ رُءُوسُ أَمْوَٰلِكُمْ لَا تَظْلِمُونَ وَلَا تُظْلَمُونَ ۝٢٧٩﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ البَقَرَةِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ condemned hoarding essential goods for private advantage as sinful.

   > “No one hoards except a sinner.” — *Sahih Muslim* 1605a[3](#ref-3){: .footnote}[14](#ref-14){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. After Prophet Muhammad ﷺ’s death, Hisham ibn Hakim invoked his warning against tormenting people when he saw farmers being abused over the *jizya* [tax historically levied on certain non-Muslim subjects].

   > “God will punish those who torment people in this world.” — *Sahih Muslim* 2613b[3](#ref-3){: .footnote}[14](#ref-14){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ taught that a deceased person’s property should pass to the heirs, while public leadership should assume responsibility for debts or dependents left without support.

   > “Whoever leaves property, it belongs to his heirs; whoever leaves debt or dependents, we will take care of them.” — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 6763[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

<p class="theme-label" role="heading" aria-level="4" id="cleanliness-shared-resources-and-animal-welfare"><strong>Cleanliness, shared resources, and animal welfare</strong></p>

45. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ forbade urinating in stagnant water and then bathing in it.

   > “None of you should urinate in stagnant water that does not flow and then bathe in it.” — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 239[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ taught that the earth itself could serve as a place of prayer and a means of purification, so worship was not confined to special buildings.

   > “The earth has been made for me a place of prayer and a means of purification.” — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 335[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ forbade striking animals in the face, including animals used for transport or labor.

   > “The Messenger of God ﷺ forbade striking on the face.” — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 5541[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ also condemned mutilating animals.

   > “The Prophet ﷺ cursed the one who mutilates an animal.” — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 5515[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

<p class="theme-label" role="heading" aria-level="4" id="leadership-learning-and-sound-judgment"><strong>Leadership, learning, and sound judgment</strong></p>

49. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ taught that people should not be appointed to public authority merely because they ask for it or are eager to hold it.

   > “We do not appoint to this work anyone who asks for it or is eager for it.” — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 7149[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ’s treatment of Abu Dharr shows that personal piety and affection did not automatically qualify someone for every practical responsibility.

   > “I love for you what I love for myself. Do not be in charge of even two people, and do not administer an orphan’s property.” — *Sahih Muslim* 1826[3](#ref-3){: .footnote}[14](#ref-14){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ taught that when God intends good for someone, He gives that person understanding of the religion.

   > “When God intends good for someone, He gives him understanding of the religion.” — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 71[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}; *Sahih Muslim* 1037[3](#ref-3){: .footnote}[14](#ref-14){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ described people who gather to recite and study the Qur’an together as receiving tranquility and mercy.

   > “No people gather in one of God’s houses, reciting God’s Book and studying it together, except that tranquility descends upon them.” — *Sahih Muslim* 2699a[3](#ref-3){: .footnote}[14](#ref-14){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. Ibn Masud remembered that Prophet Muhammad ﷺ chose suitable times for teaching because he did not want people to become bored or exhausted by constant instruction.

   > “He used to choose suitable times to teach us, fearing that we would become bored.” — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 68, 70[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. People asked Prophet Muhammad ﷺ direct questions about belief, doubt, worship, intimacy, grief, money, family problems, death, and other difficult subjects; the hadith of Gabriel itself teaches through a series of direct questions and answers.

   > “Tell me about Islam... Tell me about faith... Tell me about excellence.” — *Sahih Muslim* 8[3](#ref-3){: .footnote}[14](#ref-14){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ gave a severe warning against deliberately inventing sayings and attributing them to him, creating a religious reason to distinguish genuine reports from fabrications.

   > “Whoever deliberately lies about me, let him take his place in the Fire.” — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 107[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

<p class="theme-label" role="heading" aria-level="4" id="markets-charity-and-moral-decision-making"><strong>Markets, charity, and moral decision-making</strong></p>

56. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ forbade sham bidding used only to drive up another buyer’s price.

   > “The Prophet ﷺ forbade artificial bidding.” — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 2140[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. When Umar acquired valuable land, Prophet Muhammad ﷺ advised him to preserve the property itself and give its yield to the poor, relatives, travelers, and other beneficiaries, creating an enduring form of charity.

   > “Retain the property itself and give its produce in charity.” — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 2737, 2764; *Sahih Muslim* 1632[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[3](#ref-3){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[14](#ref-14){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}[61](#ref-61){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. The Qur’an requires washing before prayer and a full bath after major ritual impurity, making physical cleanliness part of ordinary worship.

   > "O believers! When you rise up for prayer, wash your faces and your hands up to the elbows, wipe your heads, and wash your feet to the ankles. And if you are in a state of ˹full˺ impurity, then take a full bath. But if you are ill, on a journey, or have relieved yourselves, or have been intimate with your wives and cannot find water, then purify yourselves with clean earth by wiping your faces and hands. It is not Allah's Will to burden you, but to purify you and complete His favour upon you, so perhaps you will be grateful." (**Sūrat al-Māʾidah 5:6**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿يَـٰٓأَيُّهَا ٱلَّذِينَ ءَامَنُوٓا۟ إِذَا قُمْتُمْ إِلَى ٱلصَّلَوٰةِ فَٱغْسِلُوا۟ وُجُوهَكُمْ وَأَيْدِيَكُمْ إِلَى ٱلْمَرَافِقِ وَٱمْسَحُوا۟ بِرُءُوسِكُمْ وَأَرْجُلَكُمْ إِلَى ٱلْكَعْبَيْنِ ۚ وَإِن كُنتُمْ جُنُبًا فَٱطَّهَّرُوا۟ ۚ وَإِن كُنتُم مَّرْضَىٰٓ أَوْ عَلَىٰ سَفَرٍ أَوْ جَآءَ أَحَدٌ مِّنكُم مِّنَ ٱلْغَآئِطِ أَوْ لَـٰمَسْتُمُ ٱلنِّسَآءَ فَلَمْ تَجِدُوا۟ مَآءً فَتَيَمَّمُوا۟ صَعِيدًا طَيِّبًا فَٱمْسَحُوا۟ بِوُجُوهِكُمْ وَأَيْدِيكُم مِّنْهُ ۚ مَا يُرِيدُ ٱللَّهُ لِيَجْعَلَ عَلَيْكُم مِّنْ حَرَجٍ وَلَـٰكِن يُرِيدُ لِيُطَهِّرَكُمْ وَلِيُتِمَّ نِعْمَتَهُۥ عَلَيْكُمْ لَعَلَّكُمْ تَشْكُرُونَ ۝٦﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ المَائـِدَةِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. A short report says that Muslims share rights in water, pasture, and fire. Jurists differed over the rule’s exact legal scope, but the report plainly places limits on monopolizing essential resources.

   > “Muslims are partners in three: water, pasture, and fire.” — *Sunan Abi Dawud* 3477, graded sound[4](#ref-4){: .footnote}[15](#ref-15){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. When prices rose and people asked Prophet Muhammad ﷺ to fix them, he declined because he feared meeting God responsible for an injustice involving another person’s property or life. Whatever one thinks of the economic rule in another context, the incident presents authority as limited by the duty not to wrong others, even when intervention is requested.

   > “I hope to meet God with no one having a claim against me for an injustice concerning blood or property.” — *Sunan Abi Dawud* 3451, graded sound[4](#ref-4){: .footnote}[15](#ref-15){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. When a young man openly asked permission to commit adultery, Prophet Muhammad ﷺ called him close and asked whether he would accept the same act involving his own mother, daughter, sister, or aunt. The young man answered no each time, and Prophet Muhammad ﷺ then prayed for him.

   > The young man was asked whether he would accept the act for his own female relatives, and each time answered that he would not. — *Musnad Ahmad* 22211, graded sound by Shuayb al-Arnaut[10](#ref-10){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. When Ma’iz repeatedly confessed to adultery, Prophet Muhammad ﷺ turned him away several times and investigated whether he understood what he was saying and whether the legal conditions applied before accepting the confession.

   > The report describes Ma’iz returning repeatedly before the confession was finally accepted. — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 6825[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ acknowledged that a court can reach the wrong result if one litigant presents a more persuasive case. He warned that a favorable judgment does not make another person’s property morally lawful to take.

   > “I am only a human being. You bring me your disputes, and one of you may be more persuasive than another... If I award him something from his brother’s right, let him not take it.” — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 2680[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

### <span class="num-alpha">D.</span> God, worship, hope, repentance, and the inner life
{: #god-worship-hope-repentance-and-the-inner-life }

<p class="theme-label" role="heading" aria-level="4" id="one-god-direct-prayer-and-repentance"><strong>One God, direct prayer, and repentance</strong></p>

1. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ’s central religious message was the worship of one God alone. He did not ask people to worship him, his family, saints, tribes, natural forces, or intermediaries.

   > "Say, ‘He is Allah, [who is] One,’ Allah, the Eternal Refuge. He neither begets nor is born, nor is there to Him any equivalent." (**Sūrat al-Ikhlāṣ 112:1-4**), Saheeh International[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿قُلْ هُوَ ٱللَّهُ أَحَدٌ ۝١ ٱللَّهُ ٱلصَّمَدُ ۝٢ لَمْ يَلِدْ وَلَمْ يُولَدْ ۝٣ وَلَمْ يَكُن لَّهُۥ كُفُوًا أَحَدٌۢ ۝٤﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ الإِخۡلَاصِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. The Qur’an says that God is near and responds when a person calls upon Him directly; access to God does not depend on a priestly class.

   > "When My servants ask you, [O Muhammad], concerning Me - indeed I am near. I respond to the invocation of the supplicant when he calls upon Me. So let them respond to Me [by obedience] and believe in Me that they may be [rightly] guided." (**Sūrat al-Baqarah 2:186**), Saheeh International[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿وَإِذَا سَأَلَكَ عِبَادِى عَنِّى فَإِنِّى قَرِيبٌ ۖ أُجِيبُ دَعْوَةَ ٱلدَّاعِ إِذَا دَعَانِ ۖ فَلْيَسْتَجِيبُوا۟ لِى وَلْيُؤْمِنُوا۟ بِى لَعَلَّهُمْ يَرْشُدُونَ ۝١٨٦﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ البَقَرَةِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. The Qur’an explicitly tells people who have sinned seriously not to despair of God’s mercy.

   > "Say, ‘O My servants who have transgressed against themselves [by sinning], do not despair of the mercy of Allah. Indeed, Allah forgives all sins. Indeed, it is He who is the Forgiving, the Merciful.’" (**Sūrat al-Zumar 39:53**), Saheeh International[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿۞ قُلْ يَـٰعِبَادِىَ ٱلَّذِينَ أَسْرَفُوا۟ عَلَىٰٓ أَنفُسِهِمْ لَا تَقْنَطُوا۟ مِن رَّحْمَةِ ٱللَّهِ ۚ إِنَّ ٱللَّهَ يَغْفِرُ ٱلذُّنُوبَ جَمِيعًا ۚ إِنَّهُۥ هُوَ ٱلْغَفُورُ ٱلرَّحِيمُ ۝٥٣﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ الزُّمَرِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. The Qur’an teaches that sincere repentance, faith, and reform can do more than erase past wrongdoing: God can replace evil deeds with good.

   > "Except for those who repent, believe and do righteous work. For them Allah will replace their evil deeds with good. And ever is Allah Forgiving and Merciful." (**Sūrat al-Furqān 25:70**), Saheeh International[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿إِلَّا مَن تَابَ وَءَامَنَ وَعَمِلَ عَمَلًا صَـٰلِحًا فَأُو۟لَـٰٓئِكَ يُبَدِّلُ ٱللَّهُ سَيِّـَٔاتِهِمْ حَسَنَـٰتٍ ۗ وَكَانَ ٱللَّهُ غَفُورًا رَّحِيمًا ۝٧٠﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ الفُرۡقَانِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ compared God’s joy at a servant’s repentance to the relief of a traveler in a deadly desert who unexpectedly recovers the animal carrying his food and water, saying God’s joy is greater still.

   > "God is more joyful at the repentance of His servant than one of you who finds his lost camel in a barren desert." — *Sahih Muslim* 2747a[3](#ref-3){: .footnote}[14](#ref-14){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ told of a man who had murdered one hundred people and was still told that repentance remained open to him, together with the instruction to leave the corrupt environment in which he had lived.

   > "Who can stand between you and repentance? Go to such-and-such a land, for there are people there who worship God." — *Sahih Muslim* 2766a[3](#ref-3){: .footnote}[14](#ref-14){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

<p class="theme-label" role="heading" aria-level="4" id="hardship-hope-trust-and-inner-resilience"><strong>Hardship, hope, trust, and inner resilience</strong></p>

7. The Qur’an teaches that hardship can coexist with ease and repeats this assurance for emphasis.

   > "So, surely with hardship comes ease. Surely with ˹that˺ hardship comes ˹more˺ ease." (**Sūrat al-Sharḥ 94:5-6**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿فَإِنَّ مَعَ ٱلْعُسْرِ يُسْرًا ۝٥ إِنَّ مَعَ ٱلْعُسْرِ يُسْرًا ۝٦﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ الشَّرۡحِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. The Qur’an states that God does not burden a soul beyond its capacity.

   > "Allah does not charge a soul except [with that within] its capacity. It will have [the consequence of] what [good] it has gained, and it will bear [the consequence of] what [evil] it has earned. ‘Our Lord, do not impose blame upon us if we have forgotten or erred. Our Lord, and lay not upon us a burden like that which You laid upon those before us. Our Lord, and burden us not with that which we have no ability to bear. And pardon us; and forgive us; and have mercy upon us. You are our protector, so give us victory over the disbelieving people.’" (**Sūrat al-Baqarah 2:286**), Saheeh International[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿لَا يُكَلِّفُ ٱللَّهُ نَفْسًا إِلَّا وُسْعَهَا ۚ لَهَا مَا كَسَبَتْ وَعَلَيْهَا مَا ٱكْتَسَبَتْ ۗ رَبَّنَا لَا تُؤَاخِذْنَآ إِن نَّسِينَآ أَوْ أَخْطَأْنَا ۚ رَبَّنَا وَلَا تَحْمِلْ عَلَيْنَآ إِصْرًا كَمَا حَمَلْتَهُۥ عَلَى ٱلَّذِينَ مِن قَبْلِنَا ۚ رَبَّنَا وَلَا تُحَمِّلْنَا مَا لَا طَاقَةَ لَنَا بِهِۦ ۖ وَٱعْفُ عَنَّا وَٱغْفِرْ لَنَا وَٱرْحَمْنَآ ۚ أَنتَ مَوْلَىٰنَا فَٱنصُرْنَا عَلَى ٱلْقَوْمِ ٱلْكَـٰفِرِينَ ۝٢٨٦﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ البَقَرَةِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. The Qur’an teaches that remembrance of God brings reassurance and settledness to the heart.

   > "Those who have believed and whose hearts are assured by the remembrance of Allah. Unquestionably, by the remembrance of Allah hearts are assured." (**Sūrat al-Raʿd 13:28**), Saheeh International[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿ٱلَّذِينَ ءَامَنُوا۟ وَتَطْمَئِنُّ قُلُوبُهُم بِذِكْرِ ٱللَّهِ ۗ أَلَا بِذِكْرِ ٱللَّهِ تَطْمَئِنُّ ٱلْقُلُوبُ ۝٢٨﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ الرَّعۡدِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. The Qur’an tells believers, ‘Remember Me; I will remember you,’ presenting remembrance of God as reciprocal in a personal sense.

   > "So remember Me; I will remember you. And be grateful to Me and do not deny Me." (**Sūrat al-Baqarah 2:152**), Saheeh International[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿فَٱذْكُرُونِىٓ أَذْكُرْكُمْ وَٱشْكُرُوا۟ لِى وَلَا تَكْفُرُونِ ۝١٥٢﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ البَقَرَةِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ told believers to pursue what benefits them, seek God’s help, and not give in to helplessness.

   > "Be eager for what benefits you, seek God’s help, and do not give up." — *Sahih Muslim* 2664[3](#ref-3){: .footnote}[14](#ref-14){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ warned against becoming trapped in counterfactual regret. After a loss, a believer should learn what can be learned without endlessly tormenting himself with “if only.”

   > "If something befalls you, do not say, ‘If only I had done such-and-such.’ Say: ‘God decreed, and He did what He willed.’" — *Sahih Muslim* 2664[3](#ref-3){: .footnote}[14](#ref-14){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ taught that illness, grief, fatigue, and even small pains can be occasions through which God removes sins.

   > "No fatigue, illness, sorrow, hurt, or distress afflicts a Muslim, even a thorn that pricks him, except that God removes sins through it." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 5641-5642[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. The Qur’an teaches that gratitude to God brings increase in His favors.

   > "And [remember] when your Lord proclaimed, ‘If you are grateful, I will surely increase you [in favor]; but if you deny, indeed, My punishment is severe.’" (**Sūrat Ibrāhīm 14:7**), Saheeh International[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿وَإِذْ تَأَذَّنَ رَبُّكُمْ لَئِن شَكَرْتُمْ لَأَزِيدَنَّكُمْ ۖ وَلَئِن كَفَرْتُمْ إِنَّ عَذَابِى لَشَدِيدٌ ۝٧﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ إِبۡرَاهِيمَ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

<p class="theme-label" role="heading" aria-level="4" id="intention-balance-and-sustainable-devotion"><strong>Intention, balance, and sustainable devotion</strong></p>

15. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ taught that actions are judged by their intentions and that each person receives according to what he or she intended.

   > "Actions are only by intentions, and each person will have only what he intended." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 1[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ described the religion as workable and warned that a person who makes religious practice excessively hard on himself can be overwhelmed by it.

   > "Religion is ease. No one makes the religion excessively hard upon himself except that it overwhelms him. So aim rightly and come close." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 39[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. When three men proposed perpetual fasting, all-night prayer, and celibacy as forms of superior devotion, Prophet Muhammad ﷺ pointed to his own balanced practice: he fasted and ate, prayed at night and slept, and married.

   > "I fast and I break my fast; I pray and I sleep; and I marry women. Whoever turns away from my way is not of me." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 5063[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ approved the principle that a person must give due rights to God, to his own body and self, and to his family rather than neglecting one responsibility for another.

   > "Your Lord has a right over you, your self has a right over you, and your family has a right over you. Give each its right." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 1968[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ taught that the deeds most loved by God are those done consistently, even when they are small.

   > "The deeds most loved by God are those done most consistently, even if they are small." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 6465[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ himself regularly sought God’s forgiveness and said that he repented more than seventy times in a day.

   > "By God, I seek God’s forgiveness and turn to Him in repentance more than seventy times in a day." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 6307[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

<p class="theme-label" role="heading" aria-level="4" id="mercy-accountability-and-hope"><strong>Mercy, accountability, and hope</strong></p>

21. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ taught that the mercy visible throughout creation is only one part of a much greater mercy that belongs to God.

   > "God made mercy into one hundred parts. He sent down one part among creation and retained ninety-nine parts." — *Sahih Muslim* 2752a[3](#ref-3){: .footnote}[14](#ref-14){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. A mother’s fierce care became an illustration of God’s mercy. After seeing a woman desperately search for her child and then nurse him, Prophet Muhammad ﷺ asked whether she would ever willingly throw that child into a fire.

   > "God is more merciful to His servants than this woman is to her child." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 5999[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ taught that each person will stand directly before God, with no interpreter between them.

   > "Each of you will meet his Lord with no interpreter between him and God." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 3595[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. Despair was not a reason to wish life away. Even under serious distress, Prophet Muhammad ﷺ told people not to ask for death, but to entrust the timing of life and death to God.

   > "Do not wish for death because of harm that has befallen you. Say: ‘O God, keep me alive while life is better for me.’" — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 5671[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ rejected bad omens and preferred hopeful speech. People were directed away from fearful superstition and toward good expectation.

   > "There is no evil omen, and I like a good omen." They asked what that was. He said, "A good word." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 5756[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. When Aisha asked why Prophet Muhammad ﷺ prayed at night until his feet swelled, he said that he wanted to be a grateful servant.

   > "Should I not be a grateful servant?" — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 4836[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ taught that no one earns Paradise as a wage owed for good deeds, including himself; entry ultimately depends on God’s mercy.

   > "No one’s deeds will admit him to Paradise." They asked, "Not even you?" He said, "Not even me, unless God envelops me in mercy." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 5673[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ told of a woman known for sexual sin whom God forgave after she gave water to a thirsty dog, joining mercy to animals with the possibility of forgiveness after a deeply stigmatized past.

   > "She gave the dog water, so God thanked her for it and forgave her." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 3321[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. In the Qur’an’s account of the Night Journey, Prophet Muhammad ﷺ is called God’s servant. The extraordinary event therefore does not present him as divine, while authenticated reports connect the journey with the establishment of the five daily prayers.

   > "Glory be to the One Who took His servant ˹Muḥammad˺ by night from the Sacred Mosque to the Farthest Mosque whose surroundings We have blessed, so that We may show him some of Our signs. Indeed, He alone is the All-Hearing, All-Seeing." (**Sūrat al-Isrāʾ 17:1**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿سُبْحَـٰنَ ٱلَّذِىٓ أَسْرَىٰ بِعَبْدِهِۦ لَيْلًا مِّنَ ٱلْمَسْجِدِ ٱلْحَرَامِ إِلَى ٱلْمَسْجِدِ ٱلْأَقْصَا ٱلَّذِى بَـٰرَكْنَا حَوْلَهُۥ لِنُرِيَهُۥ مِنْ ءَايَـٰتِنَآ ۚ إِنَّهُۥ هُوَ ٱلسَّمِيعُ ٱلْبَصِيرُ ۝١﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ الإِسۡرَاءِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

   The Night Journey is also reported in the hadith collections *Sahih al-Bukhari* and *Sahih Muslim*.

<p class="theme-label" role="heading" aria-level="4" id="prayer-fasting-and-a-universal-mission-of-mercy"><strong>Prayer, fasting, and a universal mission of mercy</strong></p>

30. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ taught that a servant is especially near to God while prostrating and should increase supplication in that position.

   > "The closest a servant is to his Lord is while prostrating, so increase your supplication." — *Sahih Muslim* 482[3](#ref-3){: .footnote}[14](#ref-14){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ compared the five daily prayers to bathing in a river five times a day, presenting them as a repeated cleansing return to God.

   > "If a river were at the door of one of you and he bathed in it five times a day, would any dirt remain?" — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 528; *Sahih Muslim* 667[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[3](#ref-3){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[14](#ref-14){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ taught that fasting is not only abstaining from food and drink: continuing to lie and act on falsehood defeats its ethical purpose.

   > "Whoever does not leave false speech and acting upon it, God has no need of his leaving food and drink." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 1903, 6057[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. The Qur’an describes his mission in explicitly universal language of mercy. That claim is best read beside the concrete teachings in this article concerning children, servants, debtors, captives, non-Muslims, defeated enemies, and animals.

   > "And We have not sent you, [O Muhammad], except as a mercy to the worlds." (**Sūrat al-Anbiyāʾ 21:107**), Saheeh International[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿وَمَآ أَرْسَلْنَـٰكَ إِلَّا رَحْمَةً لِّلْعَـٰلَمِينَ ۝١٠٧﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ الأَنبِيَاءِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

### <span class="num-alpha">E.</span> Character, speech, anger, forgiveness, and everyday goodness
{: #character-speech-anger-forgiveness-and-everyday-goodness }

<p class="theme-label" role="heading" aria-level="4" id="anger-forgiveness-and-gentleness"><strong>Anger, forgiveness, and gentleness</strong></p>

1. The Qur’an describes Prophet Muhammad ﷺ as possessing great moral character.

   > "And indeed, you are of a great moral character." (**Sūrat al-Qalam 68:4**), Saheeh International[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿وَإِنَّكَ لَعَلَىٰ خُلُقٍ عَظِيمٍ ۝٤﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ القَلَمِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ taught that real strength is the ability to control oneself when angry, rather than merely overpowering another person physically.

   > "The strong person is not the one who overcomes people in wrestling. The strong person is the one who controls himself when angry." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 6114[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. The Qur’an praises people who control their anger and pardon others.

   > "˹They are˺ those who donate in prosperity and adversity, control their anger, and pardon others. And Allah loves the good-doers." (**Sūrat Āli ʿImrān 3:134**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿ٱلَّذِينَ يُنفِقُونَ فِى ٱلسَّرَّآءِ وَٱلضَّرَّآءِ وَٱلْكَـٰظِمِينَ ٱلْغَيْظَ وَٱلْعَافِينَ عَنِ ٱلنَّاسِ ۗ وَٱللَّهُ يُحِبُّ ٱلْمُحْسِنِينَ ۝١٣٤﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ آلِ عِمۡرَانَ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ taught that forgiving others increases a person in honor.

   > "No one forgives another except that God increases him in honor, and no one humbles himself for God except that God raises him." — *Sahih Muslim* 2588[3](#ref-3){: .footnote}[14](#ref-14){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. Forgiveness did not require denying justice. The Qur’an permits proportionate redress while placing reconciliation and pardon on a higher moral plane.

   > "And the retribution for an evil act is an evil one like it, but whoever pardons and makes reconciliation - his reward is [due] from Allah. Indeed, He does not like wrongdoers." (**Sūrat al-Shūrā 42:40**), Saheeh International[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿وَجَزَٰٓؤُا۟ سَيِّئَةٍ سَيِّئَةٌ مِّثْلُهَا ۖ فَمَنْ عَفَا وَأَصْلَحَ فَأَجْرُهُۥ عَلَى ٱللَّهِ ۚ إِنَّهُۥ لَا يُحِبُّ ٱلظَّـٰلِمِينَ ۝٤٠﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ الشُّورَىٰ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. The Qur’an tells believers, where possible, to answer evil with what is better so that hostility can be transformed rather than simply returned.

   > "Good and evil cannot be equal. Respond ˹to evil˺ with what is best, then the one you are in a feud with will be like a close friend." (**Sūrat Fuṣṣilat 41:34**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿وَلَا تَسْتَوِى ٱلْحَسَنَةُ وَلَا ٱلسَّيِّئَةُ ۚ ٱدْفَعْ بِٱلَّتِى هِىَ أَحْسَنُ فَإِذَا ٱلَّذِى بَيْنَكَ وَبَيْنَهُۥ عَدَٰوَةٌ كَأَنَّهُۥ وَلِىٌّ حَمِيمٌ ۝٣٤﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ فُصِّلَتۡ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ taught that gentleness beautifies what it enters and that its absence disfigures things.

   > "Gentleness is not present in anything except that it beautifies it, and it is not removed from anything except that it disfigures it." — *Sahih Muslim* 2594a[3](#ref-3){: .footnote}[14](#ref-14){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ told those teaching others to make things easy, give good news, and avoid driving people away.

   > "Make things easy; do not make them hard. Give good news; do not drive people away." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 69[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

<p class="theme-label" role="heading" aria-level="4" id="speech-humility-and-social-conduct"><strong>Speech, humility, and social conduct</strong></p>

9. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ taught that a believer should speak what is good or remain silent.

   > "Whoever believes in God and the Last Day should speak good or remain silent." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 6018[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. The Qur’an forbids ridiculing others, defaming them, and calling them by degrading nicknames.

   > "O believers! Do not let some ˹men˺ ridicule others, they may be better than them, nor let ˹some˺ women ridicule other women, they may be better than them. Do not defame one another, nor call each other by offensive nicknames. How evil it is to act rebelliously after having faith! And whoever does not repent, it is they who are the ˹true˺ wrongdoers." (**Sūrat al-Ḥujurāt 49:11**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿يَـٰٓأَيُّهَا ٱلَّذِينَ ءَامَنُوا۟ لَا يَسْخَرْ قَوْمٌ مِّن قَوْمٍ عَسَىٰٓ أَن يَكُونُوا۟ خَيْرًا مِّنْهُمْ وَلَا نِسَآءٌ مِّن نِّسَآءٍ عَسَىٰٓ أَن يَكُنَّ خَيْرًا مِّنْهُنَّ ۖ وَلَا تَلْمِزُوٓا۟ أَنفُسَكُمْ وَلَا تَنَابَزُوا۟ بِٱلْأَلْقَـٰبِ ۖ بِئْسَ ٱلِٱسْمُ ٱلْفُسُوقُ بَعْدَ ٱلْإِيمَـٰنِ ۚ وَمَن لَّمْ يَتُبْ فَأُو۟لَـٰٓئِكَ هُمُ ٱلظَّـٰلِمُونَ ۝١١﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ الحُجُرَاتِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. The Qur’an forbids sinful suspicion, spying, and backbiting, comparing backbiting to eating the flesh of one’s dead sibling.

   > "O believers! Avoid many suspicions, ˹for˺ indeed, some suspicions are sinful. And do not spy, nor backbite one another. Would any of you like to eat the flesh of their dead brother? You would despise that! And fear Allah. Surely Allah is ˹the˺ Accepter of Repentance, Most Merciful." (**Sūrat al-Ḥujurāt 49:12**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿يَـٰٓأَيُّهَا ٱلَّذِينَ ءَامَنُوا۟ ٱجْتَنِبُوا۟ كَثِيرًا مِّنَ ٱلظَّنِّ إِنَّ بَعْضَ ٱلظَّنِّ إِثْمٌ ۖ وَلَا تَجَسَّسُوا۟ وَلَا يَغْتَب بَّعْضُكُم بَعْضًا ۚ أَيُحِبُّ أَحَدُكُمْ أَن يَأْكُلَ لَحْمَ أَخِيهِ مَيْتًا فَكَرِهْتُمُوهُ ۚ وَٱتَّقُوا۟ ٱللَّهَ ۚ إِنَّ ٱللَّهَ تَوَّابٌ رَّحِيمٌ ۝١٢﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ الحُجُرَاتِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. People were told to choose the best words, not merely words that were technically permissible.

   > "And tell My servants to say that which is best. Indeed, Satan induces [dissension] among them. Indeed Satan is ever, to mankind, a clear enemy." (**Sūrat al-Isrāʾ 17:53**), Saheeh International[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿وَقُل لِّعِبَادِى يَقُولُوا۟ ٱلَّتِى هِىَ أَحْسَنُ ۚ إِنَّ ٱلشَّيْطَـٰنَ يَنزَغُ بَيْنَهُمْ ۚ إِنَّ ٱلشَّيْطَـٰنَ كَانَ لِلْإِنسَـٰنِ عَدُوًّا مُّبِينًا ۝٥٣﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ الإِسۡرَاءِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. The Qur’an tells people not to treat others with contempt, walk arrogantly, or raise their voices needlessly.

   > "And do not turn your cheek [in contempt] toward people and do not walk through the earth exultantly. Indeed, Allah does not like everyone self-deluded and boastful. And be moderate in your pace and lower your voice; indeed, the most disagreeable of sounds is the voice of donkeys." (**Sūrat Luqmān 31:18-19**), Saheeh International[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿وَلَا تُصَعِّرْ خَدَّكَ لِلنَّاسِ وَلَا تَمْشِ فِى ٱلْأَرْضِ مَرَحًا ۖ إِنَّ ٱللَّهَ لَا يُحِبُّ كُلَّ مُخْتَالٍ فَخُورٍ ۝١٨ وَٱقْصِدْ فِى مَشْيِكَ وَٱغْضُضْ مِن صَوْتِكَ ۚ إِنَّ أَنكَرَ ٱلْأَصْوَٰتِ لَصَوْتُ ٱلْحَمِيرِ ۝١٩﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ لُقۡمَانَ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. The Qur’an describes God’s servants as walking humbly and responding peacefully when ignorant people address them harshly.

   > "And the servants of the Most Merciful are those who walk upon the earth easily, and when the ignorant address them [harshly], they say [words of] peace." (**Sūrat al-Furqān 25:63**), Saheeh International[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿وَعِبَادُ ٱلرَّحْمَـٰنِ ٱلَّذِينَ يَمْشُونَ عَلَى ٱلْأَرْضِ هَوْنًا وَإِذَا خَاطَبَهُمُ ٱلْجَـٰهِلُونَ قَالُوا۟ سَلَـٰمًا ۝٦٣﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ الفُرۡقَانِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ taught that repeated truthfulness develops righteous character, while habitual lying leads toward wickedness.

   > "Truthfulness leads to righteousness, and righteousness leads to Paradise... Lying leads to wickedness, and wickedness leads to the Fire." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 6094[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. Believers were to desire good for others as sincerely as they desired it for themselves.

   > "None of you truly believes until he loves for his brother what he loves for himself." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 13[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ said that despising another Muslim is itself enough to constitute serious evil.

   > "It is enough evil for a person to despise his Muslim brother." — *Sahih Muslim* 2564a[3](#ref-3){: .footnote}[14](#ref-14){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ defined arrogance as rejecting the truth and looking down on other people, not as merely wearing good clothes.

   > "Arrogance is rejecting the truth and looking down on people." — *Sahih Muslim* 91a[3](#ref-3){: .footnote}[14](#ref-14){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

<p class="theme-label" role="heading" aria-level="4" id="everyday-goodness-privacy-and-responsible-information"><strong>Everyday goodness, privacy, and responsible information</strong></p>

19. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ taught that small acts such as giving part of a date, speaking kindly, smiling, giving directions, or helping someone with poor sight can count as charity.

   > "Protect yourself from the Fire even with half a date; if you cannot find that, then with a good word." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 1417[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

   > "Your smile in your brother’s face is charity... and guiding a person with poor sight is charity for you." — *Jami at-Tirmidhi* 1956[5](#ref-5){: .footnote}[16](#ref-16){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ counted ordinary acts such as reconciling people, helping someone with a mount, speaking a good word, walking to prayer, and removing harm from a road as forms of charity.

   > "Every good word is charity... and removing something harmful from the road is charity." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 2989[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. The Qur’an requires people to ask permission and greet the occupants before entering another person’s home.

   > "O believers! Do not enter any house other than your own until you have asked for permission and greeted its occupants. This is best for you, so perhaps you will be mindful." (**Sūrat al-Nūr 24:27**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿يَـٰٓأَيُّهَا ٱلَّذِينَ ءَامَنُوا۟ لَا تَدْخُلُوا۟ بُيُوتًا غَيْرَ بُيُوتِكُمْ حَتَّىٰ تَسْتَأْنِسُوا۟ وَتُسَلِّمُوا۟ عَلَىٰٓ أَهْلِهَا ۚ ذَٰلِكُمْ خَيْرٌ لَّكُمْ لَعَلَّكُمْ تَذَكَّرُونَ ۝٢٧﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ النُّورِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. Consequential information had to be verified before it was spread. The rule protects a community from harming people on the basis of unreliable reports.

   > "O believers, if an evildoer brings you any news, verify ˹it˺ so you do not harm people unknowingly, becoming regretful for what you have done." (**Sūrat al-Ḥujurāt 49:6**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿يَـٰٓأَيُّهَا ٱلَّذِينَ ءَامَنُوٓا۟ إِن جَآءَكُمْ فَاسِقٌۢ بِنَبَإٍ فَتَبَيَّنُوٓا۟ أَن تُصِيبُوا۟ قَوْمًۢا بِجَهَـٰلَةٍ فَتُصْبِحُوا۟ عَلَىٰ مَا فَعَلْتُمْ نَـٰدِمِينَ ۝٦﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ الحُجُرَاتِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. The Qur’an tells people not to pursue or assert what they have no knowledge of.

   > "And do not pursue that of which you have no knowledge. Indeed, the hearing, the sight and the heart - about all those [one] will be questioned." (**Sūrat al-Isrāʾ 17:36**), Saheeh International[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿وَلَا تَقْفُ مَا لَيْسَ لَكَ بِهِۦ عِلْمٌ ۚ إِنَّ ٱلسَّمْعَ وَٱلْبَصَرَ وَٱلْفُؤَادَ كُلُّ أُو۟لَـٰٓئِكَ كَانَ عَنْهُ مَسْـُٔولًا ۝٣٦﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ الإِسۡرَاءِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

<p class="theme-label" role="heading" aria-level="4" id="forgiveness-reconciliation-and-humane-teaching"><strong>Forgiveness, reconciliation, and humane teaching</strong></p>

24. After Mistah took part in spreading the slander against Aisha, Abu Bakr swore to stop supporting him financially. The Qur’an called Abu Bakr to pardon and continue generosity, and he resumed his support.

   > "And let not those of virtue among you and wealth swear not to give [aid] to their relatives and the needy and the emigrants for the cause of Allah, and let them pardon and overlook. Would you not like that Allah should forgive you? And Allah is Forgiving and Merciful." (**Sūrat al-Nūr 24:22**), Saheeh International[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}; the occasion is recorded in *Sahih al-Bukhari* 4750[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿وَلَا يَأْتَلِ أُو۟لُوا۟ ٱلْفَضْلِ مِنكُمْ وَٱلسَّعَةِ أَن يُؤْتُوٓا۟ أُو۟لِى ٱلْقُرْبَىٰ وَٱلْمَسَـٰكِينَ وَٱلْمُهَـٰجِرِينَ فِى سَبِيلِ ٱللَّهِ ۖ وَلْيَعْفُوا۟ وَلْيَصْفَحُوٓا۟ ۗ أَلَا تُحِبُّونَ أَن يَغْفِرَ ٱللَّهُ لَكُمْ ۗ وَٱللَّهُ غَفُورٌ رَّحِيمٌ ۝٢٢﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ النُّورِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ described a good Muslim as someone from whose tongue and hand other Muslims are safe.

   > "A Muslim is one from whose tongue and hand the Muslims are safe." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 10[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. When a man repeatedly asked for advice, Prophet Muhammad ﷺ kept giving the same answer: do not give yourself over to anger.

   > "Do not become angry." He repeated it several times: "Do not become angry." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 6116[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ defined backbiting as saying something true about another person behind his back that he would dislike; if the statement is false, it becomes slander as well.

   > "Mentioning about your brother what he dislikes." They asked, "What if it is true?" He replied, "If true, you backbit him; if false, you slandered him." — *Sahih Muslim* 2589[3](#ref-3){: .footnote}[14](#ref-14){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. The Qur’an tells believers to repair broken relationships and reconcile people who are in conflict.

   > "The believers are but brothers, so make settlement between your brothers. And fear Allah that you may receive mercy." (**Sūrat al-Ḥujurāt 49:10**), Saheeh International[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿إِنَّمَا ٱلْمُؤْمِنُونَ إِخْوَةٌ فَأَصْلِحُوا۟ بَيْنَ أَخَوَيْكُمْ ۚ وَٱتَّقُوا۟ ٱللَّهَ لَعَلَّكُمْ تُرْحَمُونَ ۝١٠﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ الحُجُرَاتِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ taught that truly maintaining family ties means continuing the relationship even when relatives themselves cut it off.

   > "The one who truly maintains kinship is the one who, when his relatives cut him off, maintains relations with them." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 5991[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ taught that helping an oppressor means stopping that person from oppressing others.

   > "Help your brother whether he is an oppressor or oppressed." ... "You help the oppressor by preventing him from oppression." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 2444[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. A man who accidentally spoke during prayer remembered that Prophet Muhammad ﷺ neither struck, scolded, nor insulted him afterward, but simply explained the rule; he described him as the best teacher he had seen.

   > "I never saw a teacher before or after him better in teaching than he was." — *Sahih Muslim* 537a[3](#ref-3){: .footnote}[14](#ref-14){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. When a man confessed that he had broken the Ramadan fast through sexual intercourse and could not afford the required expiation, Prophet Muhammad ﷺ did not humiliate him. When food for charity arrived and the man explained that his household was the poorest in Medina, he told him to feed it to his own family.

   > "Then feed it to your family." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 5368[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. When a Bedouin promised to fulfill the obligatory prayers, fasting, and charity without adding voluntary practices, Prophet Muhammad ﷺ said he would succeed if he was truthful.

   > "He will succeed if he is truthful." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 46[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ said that he would have required tooth-cleaning before every prayer if doing so would not have imposed hardship on his community.

   > "Were it not that I would cause hardship for my community, I would have ordered them to use the tooth-stick with every prayer." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 887[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ stopped leading optional Ramadan night prayers in congregation because he feared that people might later be burdened if the practice became obligatory.

   > "Nothing prevented me from coming out to you except that I feared it might be made obligatory upon you." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 1129[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ identified lying, breaking promises, and betraying trusts as signs of hypocrisy.

   > "The signs of a hypocrite are three: when he speaks he lies, when he promises he breaks it, and when entrusted he betrays." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 33[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ told people not to hate, abandon, despise, or oppress one another.

   > "Do not hate one another or turn away from one another... A Muslim does not oppress, abandon, or despise his brother." — *Sahih Muslim* 2564a[3](#ref-3){: .footnote}[14](#ref-14){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

<p class="theme-label" role="heading" aria-level="4" id="moral-boundaries-community-and-character"><strong>Moral boundaries, community, and character</strong></p>

38. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ taught that a person’s blood, property, and honor are all inviolable.

   > "All of a Muslim is inviolable to another Muslim: his blood, his wealth, and his honor." — *Sahih Muslim* 2564a[3](#ref-3){: .footnote}[14](#ref-14){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. The Qur’an tells believing men to lower their gaze and guard their chastity before giving believing women a corresponding command.

   > "˹O Prophet!˺ Tell the believing men to lower their gaze and guard their chastity. That is purer for them. Surely Allah is All-Aware of what they do. And tell the believing women to lower their gaze and guard their chastity, and not to reveal their adornments except what normally appears. Let them draw their veils over their chests, and not reveal their ˹hidden˺ adornments except to their husbands, their fathers, their fathers-in-law, their sons, their stepsons, their brothers, their brothers’ sons or sisters’ sons, their fellow women, those ˹bondwomen˺ in their possession, male attendants with no desire, or children who are still unaware of women’s nakedness. Let them not stomp their feet, drawing attention to their hidden adornments. Turn to Allah in repentance all together, O believers, so that you may be successful." (**Sūrat al-Nūr 24:30-31**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿قُل لِّلْمُؤْمِنِينَ يَغُضُّوا۟ مِنْ أَبْصَـٰرِهِمْ وَيَحْفَظُوا۟ فُرُوجَهُمْ ۚ ذَٰلِكَ أَزْكَىٰ لَهُمْ ۗ إِنَّ ٱللَّهَ خَبِيرٌۢ بِمَا يَصْنَعُونَ ۝٣٠ وَقُل لِّلْمُؤْمِنَـٰتِ يَغْضُضْنَ مِنْ أَبْصَـٰرِهِنَّ وَيَحْفَظْنَ فُرُوجَهُنَّ وَلَا يُبْدِينَ زِينَتَهُنَّ إِلَّا مَا ظَهَرَ مِنْهَا ۖ وَلْيَضْرِبْنَ بِخُمُرِهِنَّ عَلَىٰ جُيُوبِهِنَّ ۖ وَلَا يُبْدِينَ زِينَتَهُنَّ إِلَّا لِبُعُولَتِهِنَّ أَوْ ءَابَآئِهِنَّ أَوْ ءَابَآءِ بُعُولَتِهِنَّ أَوْ أَبْنَآئِهِنَّ أَوْ أَبْنَآءِ بُعُولَتِهِنَّ أَوْ إِخْوَٰنِهِنَّ أَوْ بَنِىٓ إِخْوَٰنِهِنَّ أَوْ بَنِىٓ أَخَوَٰتِهِنَّ أَوْ نِسَآئِهِنَّ أَوْ مَا مَلَكَتْ أَيْمَـٰنُهُنَّ أَوِ ٱلتَّـٰبِعِينَ غَيْرِ أُو۟لِى ٱلْإِرْبَةِ مِنَ ٱلرِّجَالِ أَوِ ٱلطِّفْلِ ٱلَّذِينَ لَمْ يَظْهَرُوا۟ عَلَىٰ عَوْرَٰتِ ٱلنِّسَآءِ ۖ وَلَا يَضْرِبْنَ بِأَرْجُلِهِنَّ لِيُعْلَمَ مَا يُخْفِينَ مِن زِينَتِهِنَّ ۚ وَتُوبُوٓا۟ إِلَى ٱللَّهِ جَمِيعًا أَيُّهَ ٱلْمُؤْمِنُونَ لَعَلَّكُمْ تُفْلِحُونَ ۝٣١﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ النُّورِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. The Qur’an requires serious sexual accusations against chaste people to be supported by evidence rather than gossip or suspicion.

   > "And those who accuse chaste women and then do not produce four witnesses - lash them with eighty lashes and do not accept from them testimony ever after. And those are the defiantly disobedient." (**Sūrat al-Nūr 24:4**), Saheeh International[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿وَٱلَّذِينَ يَرْمُونَ ٱلْمُحْصَنَـٰتِ ثُمَّ لَمْ يَأْتُوا۟ بِأَرْبَعَةِ شُهَدَآءَ فَٱجْلِدُوهُمْ ثَمَـٰنِينَ جَلْدَةً وَلَا تَقْبَلُوا۟ لَهُمْ شَهَـٰدَةً أَبَدًا ۚ وَأُو۟لَـٰٓئِكَ هُمُ ٱلْفَـٰسِقُونَ ۝٤﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ النُّورِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. The Qur’an prohibits intoxicants and gambling, explaining that they create hostility between people and distract them from remembrance of God and prayer.

   > "O believers! Intoxicants, gambling, idols, and drawing lots for decisions are all evil of Satan’s handiwork. So shun them so you may be successful. Satan’s plan is to stir up hostility and hatred between you with intoxicants and gambling and to prevent you from remembering Allah and praying. Will you not then abstain?" (**Sūrat al-Māʾidah 5:90-91**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿يَـٰٓأَيُّهَا ٱلَّذِينَ ءَامَنُوٓا۟ إِنَّمَا ٱلْخَمْرُ وَٱلْمَيْسِرُ وَٱلْأَنصَابُ وَٱلْأَزْلَـٰمُ رِجْسٌ مِّنْ عَمَلِ ٱلشَّيْطَـٰنِ فَٱجْتَنِبُوهُ لَعَلَّكُمْ تُفْلِحُونَ ۝٩٠ إِنَّمَا يُرِيدُ ٱلشَّيْطَـٰنُ أَن يُوقِعَ بَيْنَكُمُ ٱلْعَدَٰوَةَ وَٱلْبَغْضَآءَ فِى ٱلْخَمْرِ وَٱلْمَيْسِرِ وَيَصُدَّكُمْ عَن ذِكْرِ ٱللَّهِ وَعَنِ ٱلصَّلَوٰةِ ۖ فَهَلْ أَنتُم مُّنتَهُونَ ۝٩١﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ المَائـِدَةِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. The Qur’an forbids excess in consumption, and Prophet Muhammad ﷺ also condemned wasting wealth.

   > "God dislikes for you gossip, needless questioning, and wasting wealth." — *Sahih Muslim* 1715a[3](#ref-3){: .footnote}[14](#ref-14){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. When Muadh led such a long communal prayer that a working man left the congregation, Prophet Muhammad ﷺ took the complaint seriously and rebuked him for making worship unnecessarily difficult for people.

   > "Are you putting people to trial, Muadh?" — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 705[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ told of a person whom God forgave for removing something harmful from a public road.

   > "A man saw a thorny branch in the road and removed it, so God appreciated his act and forgave him." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 652[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ linked salvation with self-control in both speech and sexual conduct.

   > "Whoever guarantees for me what is between his jaws and what is between his legs, I guarantee Paradise for him." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 6474[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. When a dispute at Quba became violent, Prophet Muhammad ﷺ personally went there to help reconcile the people involved.

   > "Let us go and make peace between them." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 2693[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ taught that ordinary estrangement should not become a permanent feud and said that the better person is the one who first reopens the relationship.

   > "The better of the two is the one who begins with peace." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 6077; *Sahih Muslim* 2560[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[3](#ref-3){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[14](#ref-14){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ taught that the people nearest to him in the next life would be those with the best character.

   > "Nothing is heavier on the believer’s scale on the Day of Resurrection than good character." — *Jami at-Tirmidhi* 2002[5](#ref-5){: .footnote}[16](#ref-16){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

   > "The most beloved of you to me and nearest to me on the Day of Resurrection are those best in character." — *Jami at-Tirmidhi* 2018[5](#ref-5){: .footnote}[16](#ref-16){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. An early report summarizes part of Prophet Muhammad’s ﷺ mission as completing or perfecting good character; the surrounding teachings show what that meant in practice.

   > "I was sent to complete good character." — *Al-Adab al-Mufrad* 273[7](#ref-7){: .footnote}[18](#ref-18){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. The Qur’an describes other people’s suffering as weighing heavily on Prophet Muhammad ﷺ and portrays him as deeply concerned for them.

   > "There has certainly come to you a Messenger from among yourselves. Grievous to him is what you suffer; [he is] concerned over you and to the believers is kind and merciful." (**Sūrat al-Tawbah 9:128**), Saheeh International[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿لَقَدْ جَآءَكُمْ رَسُولٌ مِّنْ أَنفُسِكُمْ عَزِيزٌ عَلَيْهِ مَا عَنِتُّمْ حَرِيصٌ عَلَيْكُم بِٱلْمُؤْمِنِينَ رَءُوفٌ رَّحِيمٌ ۝١٢٨﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ التَّوۡبَةِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. The Qur’an repeatedly restrains Prophet Muhammad ﷺ from overwhelming himself with grief over people who rejected the message, while making clear that he could not force their hearts to believe.

   > "Now, perhaps you ˹O Prophet˺ will grieve yourself to death over their denial, if they ˹continue to˺ disbelieve in this message." (**Sūrat al-Kahf 18:6**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿فَلَعَلَّكَ بَـٰخِعٌ نَّفْسَكَ عَلَىٰٓ ءَاثَـٰرِهِمْ إِن لَّمْ يُؤْمِنُوا۟ بِهَـٰذَا ٱلْحَدِيثِ أَسَفًا ۝٦﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ الكَهۡفِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

### <span class="num-alpha">F.</span> Parents, spouses, women, children, and the home
{: #parents-spouses-women-children-and-the-home }

<p class="theme-label" role="heading" aria-level="4" id="parents-and-family-duty"><strong>Parents and family duty</strong></p>

1. The Qur’an places kindness to parents immediately after worship of God and specifically forbids even a small expression of contempt toward them in old age.

   > "For your Lord has decreed that you worship none but Him. And honour your parents. If one or both of them reach old age in your care, never say to them ˹even˺ 'ugh,' nor yell at them. Rather, address them respectfully." (**Sūrat al-Isrāʾ 17:23**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿وَقَضَىٰ رَبُّكَ أَلَّا تَعْبُدُوٓا۟ إِلَّآ إِيَّاهُ وَبِٱلْوَٰلِدَيْنِ إِحْسَـٰنًا ۚ إِمَّا يَبْلُغَنَّ عِندَكَ ٱلْكِبَرَ أَحَدُهُمَآ أَوْ كِلَاهُمَا فَلَا تَقُل لَّهُمَآ أُفٍّ وَلَا تَنْهَرْهُمَا وَقُل لَّهُمَا قَوْلًا كَرِيمًا ۝٢٣﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ الإِسۡرَاءِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. When asked who most deserved a person’s good companionship, Prophet Muhammad ﷺ answered ‘your mother’ three times before saying ‘your father.’

   > "Your mother." He asked, "Then who?" He said, "Your mother." He asked, "Then who?" He said, "Your mother." Then, "Your father." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 5971[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. The Qur’an specifically recalls the hardship a mother bears in pregnancy and childbirth.

   > "And We have enjoined upon man [care] for his parents. His mother carried him, [increasing her] in weakness upon weakness, and his weaning is in two years. Be grateful to Me and to your parents; to Me is the [final] destination." (**Sūrat Luqmān 31:14**), Saheeh International[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿وَوَصَّيْنَا ٱلْإِنسَـٰنَ بِوَٰلِدَيْهِ حَمَلَتْهُ أُمُّهُۥ وَهْنًا عَلَىٰ وَهْنٍ وَفِصَـٰلُهُۥ فِى عَامَيْنِ أَنِ ٱشْكُرْ لِى وَلِوَٰلِدَيْكَ إِلَىَّ ٱلْمَصِيرُ ۝١٤﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ لُقۡمَانَ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

<p class="theme-label" role="heading" aria-level="4" id="consent-marriage-and-womens-property-rights"><strong>Consent, marriage, and women’s property rights</strong></p>

4. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ taught that a woman’s consent must be sought before marriage, whether she had previously been married or not.[54](#ref-54){: .footnote}[55](#ref-55){: .footnote}

   > "A previously married woman is not to be married until consulted, and a virgin is not to be married until her permission is sought." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 5136[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. When Khansa bint Khidam complained that her father had married her against her wishes, Prophet Muhammad ﷺ invalidated the marriage.

   > "Her father married her while she disliked it, so the Messenger of God ﷺ annulled that marriage." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 5138[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. The Qur’an forbids inheriting women against their will.

   > "O you who have believed, it is not lawful for you to inherit women by compulsion. And do not make difficulties for them in order to take [back] part of what you gave them unless they commit a clear immorality. And live with them in kindness. For if you dislike them - perhaps you dislike a thing and Allah makes therein much good." (**Sūrat al-Nisāʾ 4:19**), Saheeh International[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿يَـٰٓأَيُّهَا ٱلَّذِينَ ءَامَنُوا۟ لَا يَحِلُّ لَكُمْ أَن تَرِثُوا۟ ٱلنِّسَآءَ كَرْهًا ۖ وَلَا تَعْضُلُوهُنَّ لِتَذْهَبُوا۟ بِبَعْضِ مَآ ءَاتَيْتُمُوهُنَّ إِلَّآ أَن يَأْتِينَ بِفَـٰحِشَةٍ مُّبَيِّنَةٍ ۚ وَعَاشِرُوهُنَّ بِٱلْمَعْرُوفِ ۚ فَإِن كَرِهْتُمُوهُنَّ فَعَسَىٰٓ أَن تَكْرَهُوا۟ شَيْـًٔا وَيَجْعَلَ ٱللَّهُ فِيهِ خَيْرًا كَثِيرًا ۝١٩﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ النِّسَاءِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. The Qur’an assigns legally defined inheritance shares to women as well as men.[54](#ref-54){: .footnote}[55](#ref-55){: .footnote}

   > "For men there is a share in what their parents and close relatives leave, and for women there is a share in what their parents and close relatives leave—whether it is little or much. ˹These are˺ obligatory shares." (**Sūrat al-Nisāʾ 4:7**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿لِّلرِّجَالِ نَصِيبٌ مِّمَّا تَرَكَ ٱلْوَٰلِدَانِ وَٱلْأَقْرَبُونَ وَلِلنِّسَآءِ نَصِيبٌ مِّمَّا تَرَكَ ٱلْوَٰلِدَانِ وَٱلْأَقْرَبُونَ مِمَّا قَلَّ مِنْهُ أَوْ كَثُرَ ۚ نَصِيبًا مَّفْرُوضًا ۝٧﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ النِّسَاءِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. The Qur’an describes marriage as a relationship meant to provide tranquility, affection, and mercy.

   > "And of His signs is that He created for you from yourselves mates that you may find tranquillity in them; and He placed between you affection and mercy. Indeed in that are signs for a people who give thought." (**Sūrat al-Rūm 30:21**), Saheeh International[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿وَمِنْ ءَايَـٰتِهِۦٓ أَنْ خَلَقَ لَكُم مِّنْ أَنفُسِكُمْ أَزْوَٰجًا لِّتَسْكُنُوٓا۟ إِلَيْهَا وَجَعَلَ بَيْنَكُم مَّوَدَّةً وَرَحْمَةً ۚ إِنَّ فِى ذَٰلِكَ لَـَٔايَـٰتٍ لِّقَوْمٍ يَتَفَكَّرُونَ ۝٢١﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ الرُّومِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. The Qur’an uses clothing as an image of mutual intimacy and protection between spouses.

   > "It has been made permissible for you the night preceding fasting to go to your wives [for sexual relations]. They are clothing for you and you are clothing for them. Allah knows that you used to deceive yourselves, so He accepted your repentance and forgave you. So now, have relations with them and seek that which Allah has decreed for you. And eat and drink until the white thread of dawn becomes distinct to you from the black thread [of night]. Then complete the fast until the sunset. And do not have relations with them as long as you are staying for worship in the mosques. These are the limits [set by] Allah, so do not approach them. Thus does Allah make clear His ordinances to the people that they may become righteous." (**Sūrat al-Baqarah 2:187**), Saheeh International[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿أُحِلَّ لَكُمْ لَيْلَةَ ٱلصِّيَامِ ٱلرَّفَثُ إِلَىٰ نِسَآئِكُمْ ۚ هُنَّ لِبَاسٌ لَّكُمْ وَأَنتُمْ لِبَاسٌ لَّهُنَّ ۗ عَلِمَ ٱللَّهُ أَنَّكُمْ كُنتُمْ تَخْتَانُونَ أَنفُسَكُمْ فَتَابَ عَلَيْكُمْ وَعَفَا عَنكُمْ ۖ فَٱلْـَٔـٰنَ بَـٰشِرُوهُنَّ وَٱبْتَغُوا۟ مَا كَتَبَ ٱللَّهُ لَكُمْ ۚ وَكُلُوا۟ وَٱشْرَبُوا۟ حَتَّىٰ يَتَبَيَّنَ لَكُمُ ٱلْخَيْطُ ٱلْأَبْيَضُ مِنَ ٱلْخَيْطِ ٱلْأَسْوَدِ مِنَ ٱلْفَجْرِ ۖ ثُمَّ أَتِمُّوا۟ ٱلصِّيَامَ إِلَى ٱلَّيْلِ ۚ وَلَا تُبَـٰشِرُوهُنَّ وَأَنتُمْ عَـٰكِفُونَ فِى ٱلْمَسَـٰجِدِ ۗ تِلْكَ حُدُودُ ٱللَّهِ فَلَا تَقْرَبُوهَا ۗ كَذَٰلِكَ يُبَيِّنُ ٱللَّهُ ءَايَـٰتِهِۦ لِلنَّاسِ لَعَلَّهُمْ يَتَّقُونَ ۝١٨٧﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ البَقَرَةِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ taught that the best men are those who are best in their treatment of their families.

   > "The best of you are those best to their families, and I am the best of you to my family." — *Jami at-Tirmidhi* 3895[5](#ref-5){: .footnote}[16](#ref-16){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

<p class="theme-label" role="heading" aria-level="4" id="mutual-treatment-and-spiritual-agency"><strong>Mutual treatment and spiritual agency</strong></p>

11. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ explicitly instructed men to treat women well.

   > "Treat women well." — *Sahih Muslim* 1468a[3](#ref-3){: .footnote}[14](#ref-14){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ told husbands not to reduce a wife to one disliked quality, because a trait they dislike can exist alongside another they appreciate.

   > "A believing man should not hate a believing woman. If he dislikes one quality in her, he will be pleased with another." — *Sahih Muslim* 1468b[3](#ref-3){: .footnote}[14](#ref-14){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. The Qur’an repeatedly addresses believing women and men together as responsible moral agents who share religious duties and rewards.

   > "The believing men and believing women are allies of one another. They enjoin what is right and forbid what is wrong and establish prayer and give zakah and obey Allah and His Messenger. Those - Allah will have mercy upon them. Indeed, Allah is Exalted in Might and Wise." (**Sūrat al-Tawbah 9:71**), Saheeh International[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿وَٱلْمُؤْمِنُونَ وَٱلْمُؤْمِنَـٰتُ بَعْضُهُمْ أَوْلِيَآءُ بَعْضٍ ۚ يَأْمُرُونَ بِٱلْمَعْرُوفِ وَيَنْهَوْنَ عَنِ ٱلْمُنكَرِ وَيُقِيمُونَ ٱلصَّلَوٰةَ وَيُؤْتُونَ ٱلزَّكَوٰةَ وَيُطِيعُونَ ٱللَّهَ وَرَسُولَهُۥٓ ۚ أُو۟لَـٰٓئِكَ سَيَرْحَمُهُمُ ٱللَّهُ ۗ إِنَّ ٱللَّهَ عَزِيزٌ حَكِيمٌ ۝٧١﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ التَّوۡبَةِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. Qur’an 33:35 names believing, truthful, patient, humble, charitable, fasting, chaste, and God-remembering men and women side by side and gives both the same promise of forgiveness and reward.

   > "Surely ˹for˺ Muslim men and women, believing men and women, devout men and women, truthful men and women, patient men and women, humble men and women, charitable men and women, fasting men and women, men and women who guard their chastity, and men and women who remember Allah often—for ˹all of˺ them Allah has prepared forgiveness and a great reward." (**Sūrat al-Aḥzāb 33:35**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿إِنَّ ٱلْمُسْلِمِينَ وَٱلْمُسْلِمَـٰتِ وَٱلْمُؤْمِنِينَ وَٱلْمُؤْمِنَـٰتِ وَٱلْقَـٰنِتِينَ وَٱلْقَـٰنِتَـٰتِ وَٱلصَّـٰدِقِينَ وَٱلصَّـٰدِقَـٰتِ وَٱلصَّـٰبِرِينَ وَٱلصَّـٰبِرَٰتِ وَٱلْخَـٰشِعِينَ وَٱلْخَـٰشِعَـٰتِ وَٱلْمُتَصَدِّقِينَ وَٱلْمُتَصَدِّقَـٰتِ وَٱلصَّـٰٓئِمِينَ وَٱلصَّـٰٓئِمَـٰتِ وَٱلْحَـٰفِظِينَ فُرُوجَهُمْ وَٱلْحَـٰفِظَـٰتِ وَٱلذَّٰكِرِينَ ٱللَّهَ كَثِيرًا وَٱلذَّٰكِرَٰتِ أَعَدَّ ٱللَّهُ لَهُم مَّغْفِرَةً وَأَجْرًا عَظِيمًا ۝٣٥﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ الأَحۡزَابِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. When women told Prophet Muhammad ﷺ that men were taking most of his teaching time, they asked for a day of their own. He appointed one and taught them directly.

   > "The women said, 'The men have taken all your time from us, so appoint for us a day.' He promised them a day and taught them." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 101[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

<p class="theme-label" role="heading" aria-level="4" id="children-and-family-tenderness"><strong>Children and family tenderness</strong></p>

16. When a man expressed surprise that Prophet Muhammad ﷺ kissed his grandson Hasan, Prophet Muhammad ﷺ replied that a person who does not show mercy will not be shown mercy.

   > "Whoever does not show mercy will not be shown mercy." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 5997[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ shortened a public prayer when he heard a baby crying because he knew the child’s mother would be distressed.

   > "I begin the prayer intending to lengthen it, then I hear a child crying, so I shorten it because I know how his mother suffers from his crying." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 710[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ sometimes carried his granddaughter Umama while leading prayer, holding her while standing and putting her down when bowing or prostrating.

   > "The Messenger of God ﷺ prayed while carrying Umama... when he stood he carried her, and when he prostrated he put her down." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 516[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

   Children could interrupt him even in his most public and authoritative settings. On another occasion, he stopped a sermon, came down from the pulpit, picked up his stumbling grandsons Hasan and Husayn, and then resumed with them.

   > Reported in *Sunan Abi Dawud* 1109, graded sound[4](#ref-4){: .footnote}[15](#ref-15){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

<p class="theme-label" role="heading" aria-level="4" id="marriage-divorce-and-womens-access-to-religious-life"><strong>Marriage, divorce, and women’s access to religious life</strong></p>

19. The Qur’an instructs men to give the bridal gift, often called *mahr* [the marriage gift owed to the bride], directly to the woman herself.

   > "Give women ˹you wed˺ their due dowries graciously. But if they waive some of it willingly, then you may enjoy it freely with a clear conscience." (**Sūrat al-Nisāʾ 4:4**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿وَءَاتُوا۟ ٱلنِّسَآءَ صَدُقَـٰتِهِنَّ نِحْلَةً ۚ فَإِن طِبْنَ لَكُمْ عَن شَىْءٍ مِّنْهُ نَفْسًا فَكُلُوهُ هَنِيٓـًٔا مَّرِيٓـًٔا ۝٤﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ النِّسَاءِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. The Qur’an explicitly condemns the killing of infant daughters and depicts the murdered girl herself being asked for what crime she was killed.

   > "And when the girl [who was] buried alive is asked, for what sin she was killed." (**Sūrat al-Takwīr 81:8-9**), Saheeh International[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}; also *Sahih al-Bukhari* 5975[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿وَإِذَا ٱلْمَوْءُۥدَةُ سُئِلَتْ ۝٨ بِأَىِّ ذَنۢبٍ قُتِلَتْ ۝٩﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ التَّكۡوِيرِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ gave special religious honor to people who raised daughters well, linking that care with closeness to him in the next life.

   > "Whoever raises two girls properly until they grow up will come on the Day of Resurrection with me like this," and he joined his fingers. — *Sahih Muslim* 2631[3](#ref-3){: .footnote}[14](#ref-14){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. The Qur’an forbids using divorce to trap or injure a woman: a husband is told either to retain her honorably or release her honorably.[54](#ref-54){: .footnote}[55](#ref-55){: .footnote}

   > "And when you divorce women and they have [nearly] fulfilled their term, either retain them according to acceptable terms or release them according to acceptable terms, and do not keep them, intending harm, to transgress [against them]. And whoever does that has certainly wronged himself. And do not take the verses of Allah in jest. And remember the favor of Allah upon you and what has been revealed to you of the Book and wisdom by which He instructs you. And fear Allah and know that Allah is Knowing of all things." (**Sūrat al-Baqarah 2:231**), Saheeh International[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
